So, in this example, what is the probability, if we did a random sample of the cars, pick a car at random, and ask how much time it take to drive between A and B.
What's the probability that the time that car took was bigger than x and smaller than X+dx.

That's the formal definition of the probability density function,  that for each little segment (infinitely small), if you take the base (d ) and multiply with the height (f    ) tells you the probability that your random variable is in that range. ^GoXBEoV8

So every time we add the random variable, we converge their p.d.f.'s.
So the c.l.t. says that if you are gonna add n of them, as n goes to infinity, and you take off the mean (in the example we didn't took off the mean, but if we did, it would shift the function to the left, centered on 0 int the last one), then this p.d.f. of Y is a Gaussian shape.  ^oj1WzMPs

p ^mp3GKH3S

Y ^pC9Y0zGZ

(y) ^CgVn9EFw

y ^oSEkUhjm

Basically, if we have a distribution, that follow the rules, and you add enough of them togheter, the overall result will be a Gaussian shape. ^zLlCsrtd

Multivariate Normal Distribution ^m4uCdVsy

Firstly, let's get a grasp of the exponential function and its behavior, since it's going to be used quite a bit. ^wAL5UFde

To have more control of where the center of the bell is (the mean of the distribution), by shifting the x-axis, we extract the position of where we want the bell to be, using the variable μ, before raising the equation to the power of 2. ^M22tjVp8

Then, we want to change the shape of the bell. There are many ways to do that, but the most simple and powerful is to control the strength of our input variable by multiplying it with a constant σ. ^n7BNjhxT

But we can see, that this way, the spread is inversely proportional to the parameter σ, but we want it to be directly proportional. ^meg22d12

This step, is to change the inflection points of the function, you multiply it with 1/2. ^yP2yqv3e

honestly it doesn't make a lot of sense why this make a difference, so if needed, study more about it ^VvG6Hi7G

Normal and Gaussian are the same! ^Z0Uq21Ft

Lastly, to normalize the equation and make it a probability distribution  ^wNIcQLHk
